# SpotAmp Wave 1 Product Polish Plan

**Status:** Complete — 2026-10-14

**Scope:** Five starter workstreams identified in the product review
**Product focus:** Make the existing SpotCore companion experience easier to start, understand, recover, and trust.

## Wave 1 outcome

A new SpotAmp user can pair a phone with SpotCore, understand connection and offline status, find normal controls and preferences without reading technical diagnostics, and recover from common playback or download problems using plain-language guidance. The app's labels and controls consistently describe the SpotCore companion product.

Wave 1 improves the existing product paths. It does not add a new streaming backend, cloud service, discovery engine, or casting implementation.

## Wave 1 starter set: five quick wins

These are the five starter items from `docs/UX_AUDIT.md` §5. They are implementation checks, not a substitute for the Wave 1 transition gate below.

| # | Starter item | Current implementation and evidence | Remaining caveat |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Label Search's downloaded-only switch and expose its state. | `DownloadedOnlyToggle` provides a resource-backed accessible name and On/Off state descriptions; host tests cover the named/actionable switch and compact large-text header, and connected layout tests cover its 48dp target at normal/130% text. Pixel 8 host screenshots cover first-run, mixed-results, and downloaded-only-empty states. On the final-source S24, paired-online Search was checked and the downloaded-only preference remained intact. | Complete. TalkBack-specific spoken acceptance is excluded per user. |
| 2 | Make Repeat descriptions stateful. | Now Playing announces Repeat off/all/one and matching state descriptions; `NowPlayingQueueSemanticsTest.transportControlsAnnounceTheirCurrentStateAsItChanges` and the connected normal/large-text transport tests verify the full cycle. | TalkBack-specific spoken-output acceptance is excluded per user; the state descriptions and automated semantics coverage remain in place. |
| 3 | Make the mini-player's Open Now Playing action apparent. | The mini-player has a track-specific button name, visible expand arrow, and separate 48dp play/pause action. Host and connected S24 tests verify action separation; physical review confirms the mini-player opens Now Playing without starting playback. The user also confirmed audible QA playback from the S24. | Complete. TalkBack-specific spoken acceptance is excluded per user. |
| 4 | Add explicit Search retry, clear, and query-change/recovery actions. | Search exposes Retry and Connection settings on unavailable states, retains the query across the settings route, and has a named clear action. `SearchScreenJourneyTest` covers loading, results, downloaded-only filtered-empty, true no-results, clear, failure, query-preserving Settings, and in-place retry. The final-source S24 now presents counted Artists / Albums / Songs shortcuts above mixed results; tapping Songs opened the full 100-song list and dismissed the keyboard. `SearchStateScreenshotTest` covers the shortcut plus the established first-run, loading, result, empty, unavailable, and downloaded-only states. | Complete; paired-online Search and recovery verified on the final source. |
| 5 | Add a visible queue drag handle and accessible Move up/Move down actions. | Queue rows expose a track-specific drag handle and 48dp track-specific move buttons; host and connected S24 coverage exercise reorder. The user physically dragged “Sorrow” above “Bleeders” and restored the original order. | Complete. TalkBack-specific spoken acceptance is excluded per user. |

All five starter items are complete. On the final source, `:app:testQaUnitTest` passes (588 tests across 142 suites, 0 failures/errors/skips), `:app:lintQa`, `:app:assembleQa`, `:app:compileQaAndroidTestKotlin`, `:app:assembleRelease`, and `:app:lintVitalRelease` pass. The guarded connected S24 run `20261014T110721440Z-ui` passes on SM-S926U / Android 16: 72 tests across 15 suites, 0 failures/errors and 5 expected protected-profile skips. It used one ADB probe and a 600-second watchdog; QA app/instrumentation retention and notification-permission cleanup passed, QA data was preserved, and production was untouched. The user confirmed audible playback and physically restored the queue order. A fresh, explicitly authorized Companion link paired the `.qa` app; Settings reported SpotCore available, Home content returned, and a live Search query opened 100 song results through the new shortcut. Wave 1 is complete. TalkBack-specific acceptance was excluded per user.

- **2026-10-14 — Final-source connected UI suite and offline device review pass:** After the prior 180-second watchdog expired partway through the 72-test suite, the Downloads recovery assertion was narrowed to the specific row action so retained QA data cannot create duplicate “Retry” matches. The guarded S24 run `20261014T071131451Z-ui` passed on SM-S926U / Android 16: 72 tests across 15 suites, 0 failures/errors, 5 expected protected-profile skips. The run retained QA state and cleaned up notification permission; production was untouched. At this checkpoint the remaining gate was paired-online Search/recovery; it was closed later the same day by the authorized live pairing documented below. The earlier offline screen review and queue/playback evidence remain in the dated entries below.

### Live SpotCore pairing and feed compatibility — 2026-10-10

- The initial QR attempt failed because a background SpotCore process already held `127.0.0.1:4182`. After the user closed that process, SpotCore generated the temporary HTTPS pairing link and QR; the user aimed the QA phone at the monitor and explicitly approved submitting the one-time claim. SpotCore then showed a third paired device. The production package was not used.
- The first post-pair Home request exposed two server-payload compatibility gaps: track `duration` can be fractional milliseconds, and a daily mix may omit optional `colors`. The client now decodes duration as `Double` and converts at integer-millisecond app boundaries; omitted colors default to an empty list. Regression coverage exercises both response shapes. Focused QA unit tests passed.
- Installed the rebuilt QA APK in place with `adb install -r`, preserving the existing QA pairing/data. On the Galaxy S24, Home now reports “SpotCore available,” the library-refresh error is gone, and recent Home content is present. Production was not installed, cleared, or permission-modified during this follow-up.
- The QA Library UI showed interactive music rows. A row's Play action changed the player to Pause, and tapping Pause returned it to Play. This confirms the app-side play/pause control path, not audible output.
- The Settings summary still said no library details were saved. “Refresh SpotCore library” returned an HTTP error; a separate Check connection action succeeded. “Update library on this phone” then started the index worker, which stopped at offset 0 because the live page omitted `libraryRevision`; QA sync diagnostics reported the missing snapshot revision. The worker failed before committing a snapshot, and the QA library remains unindexed.
- The focused `:app:testQaUnitTest --offline --no-daemon --tests com.example.TrackCacheSyncPolicyTest` passed (10 tests, 0 failures/errors/skips). In particular, missing or unstable snapshot metadata cannot authorize pruning of existing cached tracks.
- The user approved switching SpotCore. The old `build-timer-fix\SpotCoreQt.exe` process was closed gracefully, then `D:\Documents\SpotCore\native-qt\build-mingw\SpotCoreQt.exe` was launched explicitly; the running process path confirms the intended binary (timestamp 2026-10-05, with both `libraryRevision` and `/api/v1/library/scan` markers). The updated runtime reopened the existing 43,590-track / 500-album library; no database-path override was present, and the production app was not involved.
- After the runtime switch, the user approved one fresh temporary QR and one QA claim. The S24 decoded the code, and the user gave action-time confirmation for one Connect attempt. The app later returned to Settings and the Home route successfully reported “SpotCore available,” “Connected to your SpotCore music library,” and populated SpotCore shelves; this verifies an authenticated QA Home fetch against the updated runtime. SpotCore currently shows three same-name `SpotAmp SM-S926U` registrations, so their ownership relative to this run is ambiguous; none was revoked. No replacement QR or further claim was attempted; QA data and production remain untouched.
- During the pairing screen, the app displayed its generic failure copy. SpotCore uses HTTP 400 for an invalid/expired one-use claim, while the client mapped only 401/403/404 to fresh-code guidance. The client now classifies 400 as an invalid pairing code, and the focused `ProductFailurePresentationTest` passes. The exact status behind the earlier generic message was not independently captured.
- After that, the user approved one more Connect attempt. By the time the in-place QA update relaunched, the app was on Home rather than the pairing screen, with its existing QA credential reporting “Connected to your SpotCore music library”; no extra one-time claim was submitted from the Home screen. No additional QR was generated, and the three same-name SpotCore registrations remain untouched because their ownership is ambiguous.
- The installed QA app had previously logged `LibrarySyncWorker` failing at offset 43,590 with `JsonDataException`, after reaching the declared track count. The worker now treats Music Intelligence page fetch/decode errors as optional enrichment failures, retaining existing analysis and continuing to the final stable-snapshot check; cancellation and account-isolation safeguards still abort safely. Focused malformed-enrichment and cancellation tests pass, and Debug lint passes. Direct `:app:assembleQa --offline --no-daemon` succeeded; the resulting APK metadata confirmed the `.qa` application ID, and `adb install -r` updated only that package without clearing its data. After relaunch, the S24's Advanced sync diagnostics reported Completed with 43,590 tracks processed (page 88, final page size 90); the connected Home feed remained available. No manual SpotCore rescan was triggered.
- The QA-only Advanced sync diagnostics initially retained and displayed a full signature containing encoded library-path metadata. The panel was collapsed afterward.

ADB responded again on immediate follow-up; `.qa` remained installed and `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` remained `ignore`. No QA data or permissions changed, and production was untouched. No device run was part of this validation.
- The user authorized one fresh connected S24 UI attempt (`20261011T020246878Z-ui`). All 20 ADB startup probes failed with `could not read ok from ADB Server` / `failed to check server version: cannot connect to daemon`; the saved report classifies UI as infrastructure-blocked with zero tests/suites. The attempt stopped before install or instrumentation, so QA data and permissions were not changed and production was untouched. Local-only diagnostics show one SDK ADB binary on PATH, no `ADB_*`/`ANDROID_*` environment overrides, and a healthy `adb server-status` response through PowerShell's same `2>&1` capture path; no phone enumeration was run during these follow-up checks. AOSP's Windows launcher reads a three-byte `OK\n` startup acknowledgment from the child ADB server and emits this error when it cannot read that response ([AOSP source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/system/core/+/de4505f/adb/adb.cpp#641)); this narrows the failure to local server startup/handshake, but does not prove its cause. The installed Platform-Tools is 37.0.0; official release notes list 37.0.1 (July 2026), but do not identify this exact startup issue ([release notes](https://developer.android.com/tools/releases/platform-tools#revisions)). Updating via SDK Manager is the next reasonable host-side diagnostic, not a confirmed fix. The one-run authorization is exhausted; final-source connected UI and physical acceptance remain open.
- On 2026-10-12, a fresh one-run authorization was used after a cold local `adb server-status` start succeeded on the installed 37.0.0 build. The suite then hung in its first `adb devices` call for over two minutes; process inspection showed that client had spawned a second ADB fork-server while another ADB process still owned port 5037. The same live client/process tree remained after an additional 30-second observation, so the run was interrupted as authorized. No test report was finalized; the flow had not advanced to device metadata, build, APK install, instrumentation, app-data, or permission steps, and production was untouched. The previous completed `latest` report had been archived by the interrupted wrapper, so its report/summary/log were restored from `qa-results/runs/20261011T020246878Z-ui`; the interrupted attempt itself has no persisted run ID/report and is not counted as a pass.
- That hang exposed an unbounded subprocess in `Get-ConnectedAndroidDevices`: its retry count was bounded, but an individual `adb devices` process could wait indefinitely. Each discovery call now has a 15-second watchdog, records executable path, elapsed time, PID, timeout/exit code and captured output, and stops further probes on timeout while attempting to terminate only that probe's process tree (with direct-process fallback on runtimes without tree-kill support). `qa-audit.ps1` now guards the timeout/cleanup behavior. Both edited PowerShell scripts parse cleanly and `scripts/qa-audit.ps1` passes. This fixes the harness hang, not the underlying ADB server behavior; another connected suite still requires fresh authorization.
- On 2026-10-13, the user authorized one fresh S24 connected UI run after the watchdog change (`20261013T030608559Z-ui`). It stopped before device identification, build/install, or instrumentation. QA app presence and permission fields are unset, so no QA data or permissions were changed and production was untouched. Read-only host inspection found port 5037 still owned by SDK `adb.exe` PID 38936 (`fork-server server`, started 2026-10-12 21:57 local); its `adb.log` segment ends at the startup banner without a captured handshake error, and no repository script that issues `adb kill-server` was found. Why the listener fails to answer remains unproven. The watchdog prevented another indefinite wait, but ADB startup remains unresolved; this one-run authorization is exhausted and a future connected attempt needs fresh approval.

- To honor single-attempt and correct-device constraints, `qa.ps1` now accepts `-AdbProbeAttempts` (default 20; validated 1–20) and records that value, plus an optional `-ExpectedDeviceModel`. When a model is required, the runner blocks unless exactly one device is connected, pins ADB to that device's serial, validates its model before waking or changing app state, then restores the caller's prior `ANDROID_SERIAL`. `qa-audit.ps1` guards these checks; both scripts parse cleanly and the QA audit passes.

- On 2026-10-13, the workstation SDK's Platform Tools package was updated from 37.0.0 to Google's 37.0.1 Windows archive. `source.properties` and local `adb version` both verify 37.0.1 (`adb` build `37.0.1-15733141`); the intact 37.0.0 rollback copy is at `C:\Users\cloud\AppData\Local\Android\platform-tools-backup-37.0.0`, outside the SDK package scan root.

Host inspection found no remaining ADB process or port-5037 listener; the global `adb.log` is stale from the old 37.0.0 package, so the failure cause remains undiagnosed.

- A host-only Windows Event Log check correlated the 2026-10-13 03:35:47 local failure with `adb.exe` crashing: Application Error 1000 / WER 1001 report `ucrtbase.dll`, exception `0xc0000409`, offset `0x0002da71`. The 37.0.1 executable has a valid Google signature. This proves a local daemon crash behind the handshake error, but not its call stack or underlying defect; no debugger is installed and the WER archive retained only `Report.wer`. Android's 37.0.1 notes document `ADB_USB_LEGACY=1` as a Windows fallback for the new USB backend; testing that on an isolated ADB port is a possible next diagnostic, but has not been attempted.

The isolated server was stopped, and host inspection verified port 5038 is clear with no ADB processes remaining. This narrows the failure toward the default USB-backend path, but does not establish successful S24 communication; a new connected-suite authorization is needed to validate that.

Both `DownloadsScreenJourneyTest` cases verify the actual screen and saved preference behavior.
- A further audit found Downloads had the same initial-Room false-empty risk as Playlist detail. Downloads now shows a loading state and neutral total until the first saved-track snapshot arrives; it only shows empty copy after a confirmed empty result. `DownloadsCopyTest` covers the pending state, and `DownloadsScreenJourneyTest` waits for the initial Room result before asserting its empty-state action.
- The Downloads journeys, Downloads/playlist loading checks, detail-route recovery, and Library toolbar regression are included in the 566-test Quick result.
- The route audit found that Artist and Album detail screens could continue showing saved metadata without the shared paired/offline state shown by Library and Downloads. Both now render the common connection notice and Check connection action when needed; restored detail routes also ensure reachability checking has started. `ConnectionStatusUiTest` verifies shared offline copy, retry, and omission for unpaired/healthy states.
- Detail-route audit found that album/artist child-fetch failures were swallowed as empty lists, making a network failure look like an empty catalogue. Detail-specific repository calls now preserve failures; the screens show an actionable retry notice, while existing non-detail callers retain their empty-list fallback. Repository HTTP-failure assertions and `ConnectionStatusUiTest.detailLoadFailureOffersAnAccessibleRetryAction` cover the behavior.
- Detail-load recovery and both initial-loading corrections are included in the 566-test Quick result. Playlist detail distinguishes Room's initial no-value state from a loaded empty playlist and withholds a zero-track header count until the first emission.
- A physical Settings review found the two library actions wrapping their labels at phone width. `SettingsLibraryActions` now stacks the actions below 520dp and retains the two-column layout on wider screens; Compose tests verify both arrangements, the exact labels, and compact-layout callbacks.
- Latest-source Galaxy S24 run `20261008T124328185Z-ui` passed 69 tests across 15 suites (0 failures/errors/skips) against a clean, unpaired QA profile. It includes compact Settings layout, Repeat-state and mini-player naming assertions at normal/large text, queue move-down/move-up reorder and restoration, `HomeFirstRunJourneyTest.pairingActionAppearsBeforeStationsAndOpensCameraPreflight`, and the device-local playlist journey; the previously skipped unpaired-profile cases executed. The user-approved reset cleared only `.qa` data before these clean-profile runs; QA app/instrumentation retention and notification cleanup PASS, camera remained `ignore`, and production was not targeted. Unsigned Release run `20261008T121449543Z-release` passed assembly, `lintVitalRelease`, and decompressed-content verification (`entries=541`) against unchanged production app sources. No production signing credentials or signed artifact are claimed.

- Two intervening device runs exposed test-harness issues, not app crashes: a text selector matched both the overflow item and the new Downloads empty-state action, then the aborted test left downloaded-only enabled and revealed that two journeys assumed default preferences. The test now uses the production action's unique tag and both journeys capture/restore the saved preference; the current connected run passes with the pre-existing QA setting retained.

## Roadmap after Wave 1

**Status:** Waves 1–4 are complete as of 2026-10-15; Wave 5 is in progress. This roadmap expands the earlier UX-audit outline. Each wave is rechecked against the current app, and only remaining gaps carry forward.

### Wave 1 transition gate

**Closed — 2026-10-14.** The Wave 1 exit checks are:

- [x] The five starter flows and their recovery states are audited; the reported Search scrolling friction was addressed with direct category shortcuts. One broader physical-review carryover is documented below: a Messenger bubble obscured Artist More in an earlier spot-check.
- [x] Final-source host QA, lint, QA assembly, Android-test compilation, Release assembly, release vital lint, and the guarded connected S24 suite pass. Reports are retained and failures are resolved.
- [x] Pairing, audible playback, offline use, connection recovery, and physical queue reorder acceptance are recorded below. TalkBack-specific acceptance is excluded per user.
- [x] Wave 1 status and carryover items are updated; Waves 2–5 are the next planned work.

### Wave 1 hands-on acceptance — completed

| Journey | Result and evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Pairing and connection | The user-authorized fresh Companion QR was scanned and claimed only by `.qa`. Settings showed SpotCore available and connected, and Home content returned. QA data was preserved; production was untouched. |
| Playback | The user confirmed audible output from the QA track “Grace (Interlude)” on the S24; it was paused afterward. The real-companion and connected UI suites cover playback progress and controls. |
| Offline | While SpotCore was unreachable, the saved QA track remained discoverable and played audibly. Existing offline library/download data was preserved. |
| Recovery | After the listener became available through the temporary Companion route and the QA pairing was refreshed, Settings reported connected, Home content returned, and online Search returned results. No QA data reset was used. |
| Queue interaction | The user physically moved “Sorrow” above “Bleeders” and then restored the original order. Automated and connected coverage verifies the Move up/Move down alternatives. |

TalkBack-specific spoken-output acceptance was excluded entirely per user; no TalkBack run is claimed. Broader carryover: an earlier S24 spot-check could not visually verify Artist More while the Messenger bubble covered it; automated behavior and target coverage remain in place. The temporary Companion route is hosted by SpotCore while it remains open and is needed for this live paired connection.

### Wave 2 — Search states and mini-player discoverability

**Status:** Complete — 2026-10-15. **Outcome:** listeners can tell what Search is doing, recover when a search cannot complete, understand why a result list is empty, and discover how to open Now Playing without triggering playback controls by mistake.

Work through these paths:

- Audit Search's initial, loading, successful, no-results, downloaded-only filtered-empty, offline, and request-failure states. Keep true no-results distinct from rows hidden by the downloaded-only filter. Preserve the query and selected filter through retry, and provide a clear recovery action when one exists.
- Check progress and error feedback for both the first query and subsequent edits so stale results are not mistaken for the current query. Ensure clearing the query and changing the filter have visible, predictable effects.
- Review the mini-player's open-player affordance, label, and hit area. Preserve the separate Play/Pause action and make the route to the full player apparent in the visual and accessibility trees.
- Reuse the current Search recovery and mini-player semantics when they already meet these needs; add only the missing behavior or discoverability cue.

**Exit checks:** host/Compose coverage for each state and action; compact-width and 130% text-scale layout checks; connected UI coverage for Search recovery and mini-player-to-Now-Playing navigation; a focused visual review confirming empty, loading, offline, and error states are distinguishable.

**Closure checkpoint (2026-10-15):** Search state/retry host coverage and the refreshed first-run, loading, results, no-results, downloaded-only-empty, and unavailable/retry screenshots pass; the screenshots were visually reviewed. `:app:lintQa` and `:app:assembleQaAndroidTest --offline` pass. After making the Downloads offline-mode menu selector specific, the guarded S24 run `20261015T065354018Z-ui` passed all 9 tests across 3 suites on SM-S926U / Android 16 (0 failures/errors/skips), covering Search failure-to-recovery with the query retained, retry/settings targets at normal and 130% text scale, production-shell navigation, and opening Now Playing from the mini-player without changing playback. The earlier 8/9 run exposed the ambiguous test selector; an intervening single-probe attempt was blocked because ADB did not list the S24 and made no device changes. The successful run did not clear app data; QA app/instrumentation retention and notification-permission cleanup passed, and the production package was not targeted. Wave 2 is complete. TalkBack-specific acceptance remains excluded per user.

### Wave 3 — Connection, first-run, and offline confidence

**Status:** Complete — 2026-10-15. **Outcome:** listeners can distinguish pairing, server reachability, library-sync progress, cached library details, and downloaded audio, then find the right next action when any step fails.

Work through these paths:

- Define one user-facing state model for unpaired, pairing, paired/checking, reachable, syncing, temporarily unavailable, and offline-with-saved-library states. Do not imply that saved credentials mean the server is reachable or that saved library details are playable audio.
- Trace first install through camera explanation and permission, QR scan, invalid/expired code, claim failure, successful claim, first library sync, and return to Home. Make retry, cancel, and Settings routes clear and preserve the user's place where practical.
- Trace a previously paired phone through a SpotCore outage and recovery. Explain what remains browsable, which tracks are playable offline, how downloads differ from cached details, and how to reconnect without losing the current queue or context.
- Check that Home, Settings, Search, Library, Downloads, and Now Playing use consistent status wording and recovery actions, including when a library sync fails while the server itself is reachable.
- Validate both deterministic outage fixtures and a real SpotCore journey when the server and device are available; report the scope of each separately.

Home retry has an on-device integration check: production `HomeScreen` and `MusicViewModel` use the production Retrofit client against a loopback server on the Galaxy S24, where the feed returns HTTP 503 and then 200 after Retry. This confirms the UI/client recovery path on-device, but it is not a Home retry against a running SpotCore server; that real-server-specific Home case remains a named carryover.

Wave 3 audit found two recovery mismatches. The first-run sync card always told listeners to “Check the connection,” even when the shared status model had confirmed SpotCore was reachable, making a sync problem look like a connectivity problem. Sync-failure copy now distinguishes a reachable server from an unavailable one; unknown/checking status uses neutral retry copy. Now Playing also reuses the shared SpotCore state and connection-check action only when playback has failed and the paired server is checking or unavailable, keeping healthy playback and other music servers uncluttered. Focused host tests cover the sync-copy branches and Now Playing recovery gating.

**Closure checkpoint (2026-10-15):** Final host QA passed 596 tests across 143 suites with 0 failures/errors/skips. `:app:lintQa`, `:app:assembleQa`, `:app:compileQaAndroidTestKotlin`, unsigned `:app:assembleRelease`, and `:app:lintVitalRelease` pass. Guarded Galaxy S24 run `20261015T073657275Z-ui` passed 76 tests across 17 suites (0 failures/errors, 5 expected protected-profile skips); QA data was retained and notification cleanup passed. Real-companion run `20261015T075250642Z-companion` passed Level B on SM-S926U / Android 16 against SpotCore’s Qt `CompanionHttpServer` with disposable SQLite fixtures: pairing/re-pair, 503-row and multi-page sync, favorite/rating round-trips and outage retry, metadata/artwork/lyrics/analysis/search/playlists, Downloads retry, offline playback during outage, reconnection, playback recovery, canonical history/outbox retry, and background/media-session coherence. It downloaded and removed temporary audio fixtures; the report ends with fixture favorite=false/rating=0. This is a real SpotCore implementation journey, not a run against the user’s production SpotCore library. The report’s Level-C independent audible-observation field remains BLOCKED; prior Wave 1 user-confirmed playback is separate evidence. Pairing preflight/permission denial and camera/QR acceptance are covered in the earlier device entries. TalkBack-specific acceptance remains excluded per user. Wave 3 is closed; the actual SpotCore Home-feed 503→200 retry remains a carryover, while its on-device loopback equivalent is verified.

**Exit checks:** state-to-copy contract coverage; journey tests for first-run, permission denial/retry, claim recovery, sync failure, outage, downloaded playback, and reconnection; connected-device evidence for the pairing and recovery routes; explicit confirmation that catalog metadata is not represented as offline audio.

### Wave 4 — Settings findability and Now Playing layout

**Status:** Complete — 2026-10-15. **Outcome:** common preferences and connection tasks are easy to find, advanced diagnostics stay available without crowding routine choices, and Now Playing uses its available space consistently across phone sizes and text scales.

Work through these paths:

- Evaluate Settings against listener tasks: pair/check SpotCore, refresh the library, manage downloads/offline behavior, adjust playback and discovery preferences, and find advanced diagnostics. The current sections and progressive disclosure are the baseline; change them only when task-based review exposes a real findability problem.
- Remove or clarify duplicate, stale, or implementation-facing labels uncovered by that review. Keep technical details opt-in and keep credentials and authenticated URLs out of visible diagnostics.
- Review Now Playing at short and tall viewports, including artwork, metadata, seek/progress, primary controls, secondary controls, lyrics, queue entry, and playback-recovery messages. Reduce unexplained gaps while keeping the current track and core controls prominent.
- Check queue and lyrics entry/return paths, expanded/collapsed player behavior, and layout after activity recreation so spacing changes do not break navigation or playback state.

**Exit checks:** a small task-finding review for the Settings jobs above; responsive screenshots or Compose layout checks at compact and tall phone sizes and at 100%/130% text; regression coverage for navigation and playback state; release lint/build validation after layout changes.

Initial review found Settings already exposes seven task shortcuts (Connection, Library, Playback, Downloads & offline, Discovery, Appearance, Advanced), and its existing 130%-text tests verify shortcut visibility/order, section navigation, listener-facing preference labels, and progressive disclosure. No broad Settings information-architecture change was justified. The task-finding review found one specific miss: while unpaired, the Connection shortcut landed on a tall setup summary and left the QR-pairing action below the initial view. The shortcut now lands on the SpotCore pairing card for unpaired listeners and retains the status/retry summary as its target for paired listeners. `SettingsLargeTextNavigationTest.connectionShortcutBringsPairingTaskIntoViewAt130PercentFontScale` verifies the unpaired task is immediately visible at 130% text. Now Playing’s previous screenshot set covered one device profile. New production-screen captures and layout checks cover 320×640 and 412×915 at 100% and 130% text, with core controls constrained to the visible viewport. They revealed a queue return gap: scrolling to the queue hid the only “Back to player” affordance inside the player header. The queue header now provides a 48dp “Back to player” action. `NowPlayingResponsiveLayoutTest` passes all 6 compact/tall, queue-return, and lyrics-entry/return cases; captures are `now-playing-responsive-compact-100.png`, `now-playing-responsive-compact-130.png`, `now-playing-responsive-tall-100.png`, `now-playing-responsive-tall-130.png`, and `now-playing-responsive-compact-queue-130.png`. The current-source connected suite `20261015T091301147Z-ui` passes 76 tests/17 suites on SM-S926U / Android 16 (0 failures/errors, 5 expected protected-profile skips), including `ProductionNavigationJourneyTest.miniPlayerOpensNowPlayingWithoutStartingOrChangingPlayback` with the expanded/collapsed/activity-recreation continuity checks. The state-aware Connection shortcut and Now Playing lifecycle journey therefore have current-source host and handset coverage. The full current-source host run passes 601 tests across 145 suites with 0 failures/errors/skips. `:app:lintQa`, `:app:assembleQa`, `:app:compileQaAndroidTestKotlin`, unsigned `:app:assembleRelease`, and `:app:lintVitalRelease` also pass. QA app and instrumentation retention plus notification-permission cleanup passed; the QA app was updated in place without clearing data, and production was untouched. The final visual disposition is recorded in Wave 5 below. TalkBack remains excluded per user.

A reduced-motion waveform redraw method passed on the S22+ with global animation scales set to zero; its original values (1.0, 1.0, unset) were restored. Release QA harness attempts (`20261019T020108811Z-release`, `20261019T020139080Z-release`, `20261019T020250875Z-release`) failed before project configuration while Gradle reported it could not load `native-platform.dll`; direct `:app:assembleRelease --no-daemon --console=plain` completed successfully and `verify-release.ps1` passed with the Android SDK path supplied (542 APK entries, unsigned local validation). Follow-up release QA (`20261019T022703312Z-release`) now passes through `scripts/qa.ps1 release`; the earlier startup failures are resolved. It reaches the lock-keyguard check, but the phone returns directly to Home when awakened, leaving no visible lock-screen surface to tap; the Pause step therefore remains unverified. Production signing credentials are not configured. Physical checks remain a bounded screen review, not the full route-by-route control audit. Wave 5 remains open because both phones share the same Android version and display profile, no compact viewport is available, and the full physical visual/control audit is incomplete. TalkBack remains excluded per user.

Work through these paths:

- Build a validation matrix from devices and emulators actually available: the Galaxy S24 reference device, a compact or narrow viewport, and another Android version or device profile where available. Record model, Android version, display size, and font scale with each result.
- Review typography and layout at default and increased font scale, touch-target bounds and spacing in dense rows, light/dark appearance where supported, and motion/transition behavior. Check reduced-animation system settings where applicable.
- Exercise foreground playback, notification/media-session actions, background/return behavior, and lock-screen surfaces on physical devices. Bluetooth/headset checks are deferred per the user's 2026-10-19 instruction. Separate notification/media-session evidence from lock-screen evidence.
- Complete a route-by-route visual and Compose-semantics pass for core navigation, Search, player controls, queue actions, pairing, and recovery. TalkBack-specific acceptance is explicitly excluded per user; do not add it as a release gate. If an environment or owner-scope decision prevents another check, record it as unverified with the reason and decide whether it blocks release.
- Capture final screenshots and device reports for representative normal and large-text journeys; replace stale evidence when the UI changes.

**Exit checks:** a completed device matrix with no unexplained failures; regression/UI coverage for fixes found during review; documented disposition of platform-specific gaps; final Quick, connected UI, and release QA reports tied to the same candidate source state.

The first combined-platform pass corrected a visible Warm Light contrast defect in Settings: dark-theme white labels and accents had been carried onto cream surfaces, and unavailable/not-yet-configured statuses were unnecessarily alarming. Settings now uses the active color scheme for its primary/secondary text, panels, borders, controls, and error states; the Warm Light primary is deepened to remain readable on light surfaces. Visual captures for Connection, Playback, Discovery/library refresh, Appearance, and expanded Advanced/library review were recorded and manually reviewed at 320×640 and 100%/130% text scale (`settings-warm-light-{compact,playback,discovery,appearance,advanced}-{100,130}.png`). `SettingsAppearanceVisualTest` reaches each task shortcut and captures both scales. A cross-screen scan found Search still used dark-theme white text on light surfaces; Search headers, query field, section/result labels, empty/recovery copy, and accents now use Material color roles. Compact first-run and mixed-result screenshots at 320×640 and 100%/130% (`search-warm-light-{first-run,results}-compact-{100,130}.png`) exposed two large-text issues: the downloaded-only control could be squeezed out of the first-run header, and the Songs shortcut could clip off the quick-link row. The Search header now stacks its filter at large text, and result shortcuts wrap; the visual test asserts the filter and Songs shortcut remain displayed. The latest full host/build gate passes 615 tests across 145 suites (0 failures/errors/skips), QA lint/build, Android-test compilation, unsigned Release assembly, and release lint-vital. A guarded S24 UI run passed 76 tests across 17 suites, 0 failures/errors and 5 expected protected-profile skips; the one-probe SM-S926U / Android 16 check, QA app/instrumentation retention, and QA-only notification grant/revocation passed. That handset run preceded the subsequent Search-specific Warm Light/layout changes, so those latest Search visuals have host capture evidence but not a post-change phone run. This is one device/version; additional device/Android profiles, lock-screen surfaces, Bluetooth/headset controls, reduced-animation behavior, and an end-to-end all-route visual audit remain open. TalkBack-specific acceptance is excluded per user.

### Sequencing and carryover

Run the waves in order: Wave 2 closes Search/player discoverability gaps, Wave 3 establishes cross-screen connection/offline confidence, Wave 4 refines Settings and Now Playing based on those journeys, and Wave 5 validates the combined result across devices. A finding that belongs to an earlier wave should be fixed there or recorded as a named carryover before advancing. Do not mark a wave complete from source changes alone; record its exit evidence in this plan.

Pairing scanner errors now use actionable copy while allowing a later valid QR to be scanned; Home connection labels and key onboarding/pairing language now live in Android string resources and share the same connection-state wording. Now Playing uses a considered missing-art fallback and scales down detected small source art. Settings labels for suggestion modes are benefit-focused, section shortcuts target preference cards, and the scroll state is held by the app shell when visiting another route. Added an application-ID migration proposal documenting current values, update/data risks, and the decisions required before any identity change. These changes remain source-reviewed only.
- **2026-09-22 — first-run follow-through and settings sections:** A first-time pairing initiated from Home or Settings now opens a setup card at the top of Settings. It reflects the existing sync state, offers Start/Retry for idle or failed indexing, gives an explicit next action when a completed sync returns no tracks, allows the user to continue to Home while sync runs in the background, and offers a clear Home destination on completion. The worker publishes its running state before its first network request so the card does not briefly present stale completion/idle state. Settings shortcuts now land on visible Connection, Library & offline, Appearance, Playback, and Discovery headings. These flows remain source-reviewed only; runtime timing and font-scale layout still need device validation.
- **2026-09-22 — Settings everyday/advanced separation:** Moved review suggestions and sync diagnostics after the normal connection, library, appearance, playback, and discovery controls; added an explicit Advanced section and shortcut target. The technical log remains collapsed, review tools remain opt-in, and QA Lab remains debug-only. This source change has not been build-, font-scale-, or device-verified.
- **2026-09-22 — product-language pass:** Replaced the SpotCore Home feed’s implementation-sounding “intelligence” banner with “From your library,” removed a legacy Plex name from the Player accessibility label, and limited on-device model support diagnostics to debug builds with user-facing copy. Broader strings, notification, and runtime copy audit remains open.
- **2026-09-22 — unpaired Settings navigation:** The Library section now exists before pairing and offers the same QR pairing action; Appearance, Playback, and Discovery preferences are no longer hidden behind the configured-library condition. The Library shortcut therefore has a real destination on a fresh install. Source-only; runtime checks remain outstanding.
- **2026-09-22 — sync recovery consistency:** Settings now uses the same user-facing Connecting/Fetching/Processing/Saving/failure copy as first-run setup instead of leaving the previous cached-count text during connection or echoing a sync failure detail inline. Source-only; sync/retry behavior still needs runtime verification.
- **2026-09-22 — Home connection copy resources:** Moved the Home header’s connected, checking, unavailable, cached, and offline explanatory lines into Android string resources alongside its status labels, using SpotAmp/SpotCore user language. Source-only; broader app and notification sweep remains open.
- **2026-09-22 — offline capability accuracy:** Clarified that the local index keeps library details available for offline browsing, while actual offline listening requires downloaded tracks. The Home connection descriptions no longer imply that cached metadata contains playable audio.
- **2026-09-22 — pairing claim feedback:** The claim button now pairs its progress indicator with a status label so users can tell the server is processing the device claim. Source-only; slow-network and lifecycle behavior still need device validation.
- **2026-09-22 — bounded player time formatting:** The shared duration formatter clamps negative positions, and both active and legacy player remaining-time labels now handle unknown duration and completion through one helper, avoiding malformed negative values and `-0:00`. Source-only; player state edge cases still need runtime verification.
- **2026-09-22 — download progress polish:** Replaced the visible “Fetching media stream” implementation phrase with “Saving music for offline listening,” moved the section copy into resources, and clamp displayed/progress-bar fractions to 0–100%. Retry and technical details remain unchanged; progress behavior is source-level only.
- **2026-09-22 — quiet Home reconnect path:** When reachability says SpotCore is unavailable/offline but no request error banner is active, Home now exposes a direct “Reconnect in Settings” action. Existing Retry/Settings actions remain on the error banner for active failures.
- **2026-09-22 — manual connection settings disclosure:** The QR pairing action remains primary; raw SpotCore URL and device-token entry are now behind “Advanced connection options.” Manual save/disconnect and existing Refresh/Full Update actions remain available. Source-only; 130% text scale and interaction review remain open.
- **2026-09-22 — camera permission return path:** Pairing now refreshes its camera-permission state when the app resumes after the user visits system settings, clearing the denial prompt and returning directly to scanning once access is granted. Source-only; permission lifecycle still needs device verification.
- **2026-09-22 — playlist suggestion truthfulness:** Removed the unused model-path field from ordinary on-device matching, stopped presenting the duplicate Hybrid mode as a distinct choice (existing Hybrid selections display as the equivalent on-device mode), and clarified that model-backed suggestions are experimental with a local fallback. Source-only; recommendation behavior and UI remain unverified on device.
- **2026-09-22 — Search recovery wording:** Search errors continue to use a safe generic headline and offline guidance; the recovery action now says “Retry search” and its strings are resource-backed, consistent with operation-specific retry labels elsewhere.
- **2026-09-22 — large-text Settings navigation:** Changed section shortcuts from a horizontally clipped/scrolled strip to a wrapping layout so all destinations remain visible as labels grow. This improves the 130% font-scale path by source inspection only; device layout still needs visual review.
- **2026-09-22 — Gapless Playback control:** Removed unrelated theme/equalizer writes from the toggle and wired the saved setting to Media3’s pause-at-end-of-item behavior, initialized from the saved preference. API semantics are confirmed in Android’s Media3 reference; transition behavior still needs playback/device verification.
- **2026-09-22 — Settings copy accuracy:** Corrected the gapless description so it describes playback behavior rather than promising pre-buffering, and moved its labels into resources. Follow-up state tracing confirmed the connection-status fallback is specifically the optional Plex path, so its accurate provider label is retained. Source-only; full copy and runtime review remain open.
- **2026-09-22 — Settings retry accuracy:** The unavailable/offline Settings action invokes library sync, so its label now says “Retry library sync” instead of implying a connection-only retry. Source-only; runtime behavior remains unverified.
- **2026-09-22 — pairing language:** QR confirmation now calls the URL the SpotCore address and the returned credential a one-time access key, while retaining the warning to verify the server before connecting. Source-only; pairing confirmation and security copy still need device review.
- **2026-09-22 — setup action layout:** Pairing confirmation and first-run setup actions now wrap at narrow widths/increased text size instead of being forced into one row; the QR action now says “Connect SpotAmp” rather than the protocol-oriented “Claim device.” Source-only; text-scale and pairing transitions still require device review.
- **2026-09-22 — queue action truthfulness:** The queue clear callback stops playback and removes the current item along with upcoming tracks, so both player layouts now label the action “Stop & clear.” Source-only; confirm interaction remains part of player device review.
- **2026-09-22 — download retry state clarity:** A transient worker failure now displays “Retrying automatically” while WorkManager schedules another attempt; the app stores terminal failure details and exposes manual Retry only after automatic retries are exhausted. Source-only; retry/backoff and cancellation still need runtime validation.
- **2026-09-22 — playback recovery copy:** Playback failures now use cause-neutral resource copy rather than assuming a network problem, and the visible Retry label is shared with the accessible play control description. Queue rebuild remains the retry operation; source-only, playback-error scenarios still need device verification.
- **2026-09-22 — integration verification:** With Android Studio JBR (Java 17+), `:app:testDebugUnitTest` and `:app:assembleDebug` both passed. The first test attempt exposed a missing `positionInParent` import in Settings section navigation; adding it cleared Kotlin compilation and the full unit task passed. `git diff --check` still reports pre-existing trailing whitespace across the broadly modified worktree; none was mass-cleaned. A connected Android device is visible, but no install/instrumented journey has been run, so visual, pairing, playback, and font-scale acceptance remains open.
- **2026-09-22 — download retry regression coverage:** Extracted the automatic-retry boundary into a pure policy and added unit tests for retrying through the configured limit and failing after it. The updated full `:app:testDebugUnitTest` suite passed, and `:app:assembleDebug` passed again with the policy included. A KSP AWT/IDE null-pointer message appeared during test compilation but did not fail the task; SDK XML and deprecation warnings remain. Device-only acceptance is still open.
- **2026-09-22 — release-variant verification:** `:app:assembleRelease` passed, including release Kotlin compilation and lint-vital checks, with release signing intentionally unavailable because no signing credentials were configured. QA Lab entry and screen are both guarded by `BuildConfig.DEBUG`; the release build is unsigned validation, not a shippable artifact. No device/UI review was performed.
- **2026-09-22 — system Next-control accuracy:** Added one tested availability policy shared by Now Playing and the media notification: Next is available only when a following queue item exists or repeat-all can wrap, and never for a single-track queue. The notification now observes track, playback, queue, index, and repeat changes and omits both its Next action and MediaSession capability when unavailable. `:app:testDebugUnitTest` and `:app:assembleDebug` passed with this change; notification behavior still needs device validation.
- **2026-09-22 — Downloads large-text resilience:** The Download queue / Retry all failed header now wraps instead of forcing both controls into a single row, and those touched labels are resource-backed. `:app:compileDebugKotlin` passed; 130% screen review remains open.
- **2026-09-22 — queue drag reorder correctness:** Large drags now calculate sequential adjacent moves against the updated index, clamp at queue boundaries, and preserve only sub-row drag remainder. The threshold scales with display density; stable occurrence keys preserve distinct rows during reorder; explicit move buttons remain the accessible alternative. All three `QueueReorderPolicyTest` cases passed, and `:app:testDebugUnitTest` plus `:app:assembleDebug` completed successfully. KSP emitted its known AWT/IDE null-pointer message without failing the build; runtime gesture review remains open.

Home copy audit found no obvious implementation jargon in the inspected files. `:app:compileDebugKotlin` passed after correcting the generated resource import; the build still reports existing deprecation/named-parameter warnings.

## Workstreams and completion checks

### 1. Guided first-run setup and pairing

**Current friction:** An unconfigured user reaches Home and must find Settings. Pairing exists, but the first-run purpose, permission explanation, failure recovery, and next step are not presented as one journey.

**Plan:**

- Detect the unpaired, paired-but-unreachable, reachable, and offline-with-cache situations from the existing companion configuration and sync/playback signals.
- Replace the unconfigured Home dead end with a short welcome and “Connect to SpotCore” path. **First-run entry physically verified:** on a clean QA profile, the pairing card appears immediately below the Home header and above Stations; its CTA opens the camera-purpose explanation, and Cancel does not request camera access. The isolated `.qa` flow then passed live QR scan/claim, indexing of all three fixture tracks, Settings ready state, and the first-pair handoff to connected Home on Galaxy S24 / Android 16 (2026-10-01; evidence in the live-pairing report above).
- Explain that SpotCore must be running and that pairing scans its Add Device QR code. Explain camera use immediately before requesting camera access. **Implemented; denial recovery physically verified on SM-S926U / Android 16 (2026-10-01):** Android showed the permission prompt only after Continue; the first denial exposed Retry, Cancel, and app Settings, and Retry reopened the prompt. After a second denial, Android blocked re-prompting; the app replaced the ineffective retry with an explanation and Settings-only recovery. Returning from Settings and canceling/reopening the pairing dialog preserved that state. The final QA permission check remained `granted=false` (`USER_SET|USER_FIXED`); the production package was untouched. Preflight layout also passed connected-device checks at 100% and 130% text. An earlier same-day attempt was inconclusive because Android granted camera with `ONE_TIME`; the later authorized run closes the live deny→retry/settings gate. The live QR scan itself passed on 2026-10-01.
- Reuse the existing QR decoder and claim flow. Show the server identity and requested device name before claim; show progress, actionable failures, and a completion state. **Verified in Compose and live device flow:** pairing confirmation displays the SpotCore address and editable device name before Claim, never renders the one-time secret, and keeps Scan again/Cancel/Claim, progress, and failure recovery reachable at 320 dp width and 130% text. On Galaxy S24, the isolated `.qa` package scanned a real SpotCore QR and the real claim endpoint returned HTTP 200; the pairing report records the result.
- After pairing, lead into library selection/indexing and show progress, what can already be played, and what remains pending. Provide a clear route to Home when setup is done. **Verified on device:** the live claim selected “SpotCore Library,” indexed three fixture tracks, showed the Settings ready state, and “Continue to Home” opened a connected Home. The setup card still reflects intermediate sync states, preserves browsable saved tracks during refresh, and provides retry/background continuation; empty-library guidance/retry and saved-count visibility remain Compose-tested.
- Offer “Not now” and preserve the ability to browse cached music when reconnecting or offline.

**Done when:** Fresh install through successful pair reaches a populated or clearly syncing Home without requiring the user to discover Settings. Denied camera permission, invalid/expired QR, unreachable server, claim failure, no library, and retry all have a clear next action. Returning paired users are not forced through onboarding again.

**Dependencies:** Share the connection-state vocabulary and user-facing error mapping in workstream 3. Reuse current pairing APIs and settings storage.

### 2. Settings information architecture

**Current friction:** Settings combines pairing, device readiness, sync logs, metadata review, collection suggestions, AI configuration, and ordinary preferences in one long screen. User settings and support diagnostics compete for attention.

**Plan:**

- Make connection status, paired device, library, and recovery actions the first section.
- Group normal preferences into compact sections: Playback, Downloads & offline, Discovery, and Appearance. **Implemented and physically reviewed:** Visible headings group Connection, Library, Playback, Downloads & offline, Discovery, Appearance, and Advanced, in the same order as section shortcuts. The physical Galaxy S24 review covered 100% and 130% text; shortcut alignment was subsequently checked at 130%. TalkBack review remains open.
- Keep Discovery provider options as one clear, accessible choice each. **Verified:** all three provider options expose a single RadioButton role and full-row action; their RadioButton indicators are non-clickable to avoid nested duplicate controls. Production Compose coverage verifies roles, actions, and selection state.
- Put metadata review and collection suggestions under a clearly named library tools area, with their review-only behavior explained. **Implemented and verified:** the Advanced “Library review tools” disclosure states that checks and suggestions are read-only; metadata issues and local-only collection ideas are contained inside it, with a SpotCore-specific no-change footer. Collection names and track counts use a resource-backed singular/plural row. `SettingsLargeTextNavigationTest.advancedLibraryToolsRefreshCollectionSuggestionsAfterSameCountLibraryEdit` seeds the app database, opens the disclosure, verifies the non-empty suggestion/count/footer, then updates collection tags without changing track count and verifies the displayed suggestion refreshes. Both review panels now share one cached-track read.
- Move raw sync counters, signatures, prefixes, event logs, and QA Lab behind Advanced / Diagnostics. Keep QA Lab restricted to QA/debug builds. **Implemented and verified:** technical details and library review tools follow the everyday settings in an explicit Advanced section; raw details remain collapsed until opened, with the disclosure exposing button role and expanded/collapsed state. QA Lab remains debug-only at the bottom. Physical layout evidence and automated QA-build checks are recorded below; TalkBack review remains open.
- Add section navigation or progressive disclosure that works with the existing phone layout and preserves scroll/route state when returning from a subpage. **Implemented and physically checked:** Horizontal section shortcuts, collapsed tools/diagnostics disclosure, and app-shell-owned Settings scroll state are in place. The Settings surface was reviewed top-to-bottom at 100% and 130%; subsequent shortcut adjustment puts the selected content in view and was checked at 130%. Paired-library behavior and TalkBack remain separate acceptance items.
- Ensure primary copy uses user concepts; retain technical details as expandable support information.

**Done when:** A user can identify the connection and reconnect path at the top, reach common preferences without scrolling through diagnostics, and open technical logs only intentionally. At 130% font scale, all section names and controls remain readable and navigable.

**Dependencies:** Use the same connection states and language as onboarding and errors. Keep existing settings behavior and persistence intact while regrouping controls.

### 3. Plain-language connection, playback, and download recovery

**Current friction:** Some failures surface transport/provider detail directly, including Plex and HTTP status wording. Connection state can read differently between Home and Settings, and repeated failed downloads overwhelm the Downloads screen.

**Plan:**

- Define one small user-facing state model for SpotCore: Not paired, Connecting, Connected, Temporarily unavailable, and Offline library available. Derive it from existing settings, sync, network, and playback signals; do not infer reachability merely from saved credentials. **Verified:** A status-only probe runs on IO when Settings is restored directly or the user retries, so Home need not be active. The restored-Settings test covers 503→204 and verifies the terminal Connected state; existing coverage also verifies unavailable/offline presentation.
- Give each failure a short user message, a primary recovery action, and optional expandable technical detail. Keep tokens and sensitive values out of both messages and diagnostics. **In progress:** the shared connection/pairing/download failure copy now resolves from Android resources and identifies SpotCore consistently for connection failures; sync diagnostics redact tokens, and download failure details are redacted at the Room write boundary before persistence. Playlist creation in the Library, the active “Add to playlist” context menu, and the shared Downloads/detail chooser now keeps failures visible with a retry path and retains the draft. Home, Search, Settings, first-run, Now Playing, Downloads, pairing, and the Library picker/playlist error surfaces expose polite error/live-region semantics where appropriate. The Home, Settings, and Now Playing technical-detail disclosures plus Downloads failure details expose `Collapsed`/`Expanded` state; the expandable Downloads playlist header also identifies itself as a Button and announces its current state. Cross-surface and physical recovery review remains open.
- Normalize retry behavior and labels across Home, Settings, Search, Now Playing, and Downloads. **Verified for Home and Search:** `HomeFeedRetryTest` renders production `HomeScreen`, injects a failed companion-feed request, taps the production Retry callback, and verifies the request repeats and Connected/error-cleared state returns (2 tests, `20261005T024207067Z-now-playing`; host Compose integration). The connected UI suite verifies the Home Retry action at 130% text scale, and `HomeFeedRetryDeviceTest.productionHomeRetryRecoversAfterRealHttp503OnDevice` now exercises production HomeScreen/Retrofit on the Galaxy S24 against a loopback 503→200 server (`20261008T084400686Z-ui`); a live SpotCore Home retry remains open. Search recovery copy names SpotCore and accurately offers browsing saved details when available; the 9-test `SearchCopyTest` covers both cache states, polite error semantics, Retry callback, and Retry visibility at 130% text scale in a short viewport. A physical S24 spot-check also returned cached QA artist/album/song results with the shared offline notice at 100%, and the empty Search/offline copy fit at 130%. The SearchScreen call site binds Retry to rerunning the current query. Existing Settings 503→204 recovery and Downloads failed-only retry coverage remain separate checks.
- On Downloads, group failures and provide Retry all failed and Clear failed items where safe; keep per-item Retry/Cancel and expose technical detail only on demand. **Verified:** Retry all filters to failed records (not active downloads) and is omitted for a single failure so per-item Retry remains the clear action. The Clear failed items menu action appears only when failures exist and deletes failed records only; its regression confirms active/completed downloads remain and the menu dismisses (`DownloadFailureDetailsTest`, 8 cases). Removing an album or playlist’s offline tracks requires confirmation, names the collection/count, and explains the phone-only effect; confirm/cancel and singular/plural copy are covered by `DownloadsCopyTest` and `DeleteAllDownloadsDialogTest`.
- Distinguish unavailable server, expired/invalid pairing, missing media, network interruption, and local storage failure where current responses support that distinction. Use a useful generic message where they do not. **Verified:** `ProductFailurePresentationTest` covers auth and missing-media responses, rate limits and 5xx service failures, pairing-code expiry and service failure, DNS/routing/timeouts, empty downloads, write failures, and no-space errors; focused suite passes (15 tests, 0 failures/errors/skips).
- Preserve useful cached library and completed downloads through connection loss.

**Done when:** The same underlying connection has the same user-visible state throughout the app. Common connection, playback, and download failures say what happened and what to do. No routine error headline exposes provider implementation names, raw exception text, or HTTP codes; technical causes remain available for support when useful.

**Dependencies:** This workstream establishes shared state/copy contracts for onboarding, Settings, and the player. It should be implemented before those surfaces diverge into separate error language.

### 4. Finish player details and remove inactive controls

**Current friction:** The player can expose `-0:00`, upscale poor artwork, truncate generated commentary, or show queue controls for a one-track queue. Cast icons suggest an action that is not implemented; the Library cast icon currently has an empty click handler.

**Plan:**

- Correct progress/duration formatting for unknown, zero, completed, and valid durations; avoid negative zero and invalid ranges. **Verified:** unknown duration displays `--:--`; elapsed accessibility time is clamped to the known track duration; unknown-duration drag gestures do not issue a seek; negative/overrun/completed/zero/unknown formatting cases are covered. Focused `NowPlayingSeekSemanticsTest` passes (5 tests).
- Improve artwork presentation when source art is small or missing: use a deliberate fallback and avoid making pixelated art dominate the player. **Verified in Compose and connected device checks:** production Now Playing renders its artwork-unavailable icon/copy without an artwork URL; threshold tests cover small versus full-presentation art. The mini-player now uses the neutral SpotAmp album tile for both absent and failed artwork, verified by connected pixel checks on Galaxy S24; the shared Coil request also uses that tile while loading/failing. Fallback labels are resource-backed. Real-cover physical image-quality review remains.
- Keep generated commentary optional and bounded if it is present in a future listener-facing player. **Reviewed:** The current Now Playing production screens render no generated commentary or “Local DJ” label; `LocalDjBlurbService` currently has no production call sites, so there is no active UI behavior to change in this wave.
- Align queue labels and counts with behavior: “Now playing” for the active item; “Up next” only for queued items. Hide or disable Next/reorder operations when they cannot do useful work. **Verified:** added final-item coverage for disabled Next with Repeat Off and enabled wrap with Repeat All; the disabled Next icon now uses a muted tint rather than appearing active. Its state description explains when no next track is available. The lazy queue key lookup is guarded against queue restoration changing the cached key list mid-layout. Active queue rows expose a Button role for tap-to-play while per-track Move and Options controls remain independently reachable; the focused queue-row regression passes (1/1). Focused `NowPlayingQueueSemanticsTest` also covers queue labels and boundaries.
- Keep reorder controls visibly distinguishable on the dark queue surface, including their disabled boundary state. **Verified on Galaxy S24:** explicit on-surface tint fixed the near-black arrows; available and disabled states are both legible in the physical queue view.
- Keep debug-only QA tools readable in the active theme. **Verified on device:** the surface uses readable `onBackground` contrast, fixture/action buttons wrap without vertical clipping, the content scrolls to the full runtime card, and sync state is listener-readable rather than a Kotlin object identity. See the dated physical evidence above.
- Remove cast controls from surfaces where casting is not supported, including the inert Library control. Keep the scope to working controls already supported by the app. **Verified:** removed placeholders from Home, Library, Downloads, and artist details; a current listener-UI and string-resource search found no remaining cast/Chromecast/AirPlay affordance.
- Retain and recheck the player’s existing play/pause, seek, shuffle, repeat, favorite, and queue behavior. The visual-mode selector is also verified as a Button whose accessible name follows Artwork/Spectrum/Waveform/Radial mode changes (`PlaybackArtworkPolicyTest`, 4 tests); physical signal-motion review remains open.

**Done when:** No invalid time value appears, missing/low-resolution art has a considered presentation, single-track queue actions are accurate, and every visible control either performs its labeled action or is intentionally absent. Large text and compact layouts do not clip core transport controls.

**Dependencies:** Use the workstream 3 playback error model for Retry and unavailable states. Coordinate queue labels with current Media3 queue semantics.

### 5. Consistent product language and visible identity

**Current friction:** User-visible screens mix SpotAmp, SpotCore, Plex, backend, LocalAI, CLAP/taxonomy, and other internal or provider terms. The repository README is still an AI Studio starter template, the app ID carries a legacy Plex project name, and release metadata remains at version 1.0 / code 1.

**Plan:**

- Establish a short copy glossary: SpotAmp is the phone app; SpotCore is the companion server; “music library,” “offline,” and “connection” are user terms. Use provider/algorithm names only in Advanced diagnostics or where a user must identify a configured service.
- Replace implementation-oriented headlines such as “CLAP and taxonomy-powered discovery” with a truthful benefit-focused line. Avoid promising capabilities not established by the active backend.
- Move user-facing strings into Android resources as touched, beginning with shared connection/error/onboarding copy; avoid an unrelated full-app localization rewrite in this wave.
- Replace the starter README with SpotAmp purpose, SpotCore setup, supported flows, troubleshooting, development/QA commands, and release-build notes. **Done:** The README documents those paths and the legacy application-ID migration caution; hardware validation instructions remain in device QA docs.
- Prepare a separate release identity/versioning change proposal. Change `applicationId`, package namespace, signing identity, or public version only with an explicit migration/release decision because these can affect installed builds and update compatibility. **Done as a proposal:** see [APP_IDENTITY_MIGRATION.md](APP_IDENTITY_MIGRATION.md); no identity values were changed.

**Done when:** A user-facing copy sweep finds no unexplained “backend,” Plex implementation leakage in the SpotCore path, internal AI/provider names, raw protocol terminology, or starter-template README language. Product naming is consistent across Home, Settings, errors, notifications, and onboarding. Any package/version migration decision is documented before making the change.

**Dependencies:** Coordinate the glossary with workstreams 1–4. Start the README and copy audit in parallel; defer identity migration until distribution/update requirements are known.

## Suggested implementation order

| Order | Work | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Agree on SpotAmp/SpotCore glossary and connection/error state names | Gives every surface the same vocabulary. |
| 1 | Workstream 3: shared connection and recovery behavior | Onboarding, Settings, and player need consistent state and retry guidance. |
| 2 | Workstream 1: first-run setup | Turns the current unconfigured state into a complete entry path. |
| 3 | Workstream 2: Settings structure | Makes connection management and preferences findable after setup. |
| 4 | Workstream 4: player details and inactive controls | Polishes the central listening surface and removes misleading affordances. |
| 5 | Workstream 5: copy sweep and README | Applies the glossary across the product after the new flows are in place. |

The glossary and README can begin earlier. The order above is the dependency order for product behavior and final copy review.

## Wave-level acceptance review

- Walk fresh install → pair → library/index progress → play, including camera denial and retry paths. **Happy path and denial recovery verified:** On SM-S926U / Android 16, the isolated `.qa` app scanned a live SpotCore QR, claimed it, indexed three tracks, showed the Settings ready state, and completed the first-pair handoff to a connected Home; the real companion gate also covers playback. The QA permission run physically verified first-denial → Retry → second denial → Settings-only recovery when Android blocks another prompt; the app remained camera-denied throughout the final check. Production was untouched.
- Walk paired → server unavailable → reconnect → resume, with and without cached music. **Verified in the real companion gate:** A downloaded track plays while SpotCore is stopped and resumes after SpotCore restarts; the uncached stream separately exposes a retryable failure and recovers after restart.
- Trigger representative download and playback failures; confirm the primary action and optional technical detail. **Verified:** Device review covered failed Downloads, per-item/bulk retry and opt-in details; the real-companion journey now taps the per-item Retry control and verifies a completed local file; connected playback tests cover unsupported local audio and real stream outage recovery.
- Review Settings top-to-bottom at normal and 130% font scale; confirm that QA-only tools do not appear in release builds. **Verified:** Physical review covered 100%/130%; QA Lab is debug-only, release BuildConfig excludes QA, and the release artifact verifier passed.
- Review Now Playing and Queue for known duration, unknown duration, missing/low-resolution art, single-track, and multi-track states. **Verified in Compose/device evidence:** Production captures cover the named player/queue states, and the shared failed-artwork fallback now passes an on-device pixel assertion. The live Home screenshot also exposed and drove the fix for its former warning-icon fallback. The Galaxy S24 queue/transport review covered 130% text; Move up/Move down semantics and rendered-order behavior are covered, including a device-injected long-press reorder. The current QA build also passed a physical mini-player → Now Playing → queue navigation and button-reorder check; original order was restored and logged in the 2026-10-08 evidence. Now Playing also has an explicit visual-mode selector; Waveform/Radial are implemented and use throttled decoded PCM while selected, with host tests for tone/silence analysis and mode/canvas selection. A physical signal-motion check remains open. Real-cover visual quality and unaided human tactile drag review remain open; spoken TalkBack review is out of scope per the owner decision recorded in `docs/UX_AUDIT_POST_IMPLEMENTATION.md`.
- Verify all visible buttons have working, accurately named actions and check user-facing copy across Home, Search, Library, Downloads, Settings, notifications, and pairing. **Partial:** Connected UI tests and the targeted listener-copy sweep pass. This pass also reviewed active notifications and playlist details; new Compose coverage verifies playlist-specific action names, saved-state behavior, plural counts, and the absence of inactive actions for empty generated playlists. Full Search Results options now open type-correct artist/album/track context menus; Compose coverage verifies all three targets. Library track menus and Artist/Album detail actions now name the track or collection they affect. Artist detail no longer advertises inline lists as “See all” actions or static pager dots, and its style rows no longer label playback as “Options.” A complete screen-by-screen control audit remains in progress. Spoken TalkBack review is deferred per the recorded owner decision.
  The 2026-10-08 physical Galaxy S24 spot-check also verified cached Search results and Library/Artist navigation at 100%, with the Search empty/offline state readable at 130%. The floating Messenger bubble obscures Artist More, so that physical control remains unverified.
  The latest 2026-10-01 control pass adds target-specific Library artist and Now Playing options, truthful Library view-mode action names, and resource-backed Settings theme copy; focused Library/Settings/contract suites pass (27 tests total), with `:app:assembleQa` successful.
  The current source-level semantics sweep found decorative artwork images whose descriptions repeated the adjacent visible artist/album/track title in Library, Home, Search, Downloads, the queue, mini-player, and context-menu rows. That redundancy is now removed without changing image content; the mini-player’s parent action still identifies the current track and artist.
  A source and Compose-bounds pass also found compact 32dp actions in Downloads and playlist track rows. Shared feedback icon buttons now enforce a 48dp minimum, and the specific download add/options and playlist removal actions assert 48dp bounds; focused checks and the full 517-test quick suite pass. Physical dense-row layout and the remaining app-wide target audit are still open.
  Since that earlier scan, explicit 48dp targets and focused bounds checks have also been added for Search result More, shared Library track-row favorite/options/rating, queue-row More and Move Up/Down, and album/playlist header actions. The phone check exposed default 40dp queue move targets, which are now explicit 48dp targets. Latest device evidence checks those rows at 320dp and normal/130% text (26 connected tests, `20261006T225140822Z-ui`); the full quick gate passes 522 host tests across 134 suites (`20261006T225330257Z-quick`). Physical dense-row review on other screen sizes and the complete app-wide audit of remaining/default-sized IconButtons are still open.
  A 2026-10-07 continuation of that source-and-bounds pass found synced lyric seek rows measured 43dp; seekable lines now enforce a 48dp minimum. Host and connected checks confirm the bound at 320dp under normal and 130% text scale. The current reports pass 522 host tests across 134 suites and 28 connected tests across 14 suites on Galaxy S24 / Android 16 (`20261007T071907133Z-quick`, `20261007T072715211Z-ui`). This closes only that target gap; the full screen-by-screen control/layout audit remains open.

The five intervals now expose one selected `RadioButton` action apiece; host and connected regressions check role, selected state, unique action count, callback, and 320dp fit at normal/130% text scale. Current full reports pass 522 host tests and 30 connected tests (`20261007T074055780Z-quick`, `20261007T075011164Z-ui`). This is one more closed audit item, not whole-app completion.

The current full `:app:testQaUnitTest --offline --no-daemon --max-workers=1 --no-configuration-cache` run passes 419 tests across 119 suites (0 failures/errors/skips), including resource-backed station-builder, Smart Playlist, and Settings copy checks, collection-suggestion pluralization, offline collection-removal confirmation, playlist-creation failure/retry coverage, and duplicate-artwork-semantics regressions; `:app:assembleQa` and `:app:assembleRelease` also pass, including release lint-vital. ## Explicitly deferred

- New recommendation, AI, radio, theme, or social features.
- Chromecast or other casting support.
- Changing backend or playback architecture as part of visual polish.
- Renaming the installed Android package/application ID before update compatibility is decided.
- Broad refactors of large Compose files beyond what is needed to implement these workstreams safely.

- **2026-10-18 — Wave 5 pause-aware visualizer handset follow-up:** Visualizer PCM capture now follows Media3's actual playing state through `PlaybackVisualizerPlaybackGate`, shared by `PlaybackManager` and the focused Android test. Pausing freezes the current frame and prevents an in-flight analysis from publishing after pause; clearing the queue, natural completion, and playback errors reset the visualizer. `PlaybackVisualizerCaptureTest` passes all 5 cases, and `:app:assembleQa` plus `:app:assembleQaAndroidTest` pass. `PlaybackMotionDeviceTest.visualizerFreezesOnPauseAndResumesWithLocalTone` passed on the guarded SM-S926U / Android 16 with a real ExoPlayer, Media3's `TeeAudioProcessor`, and a generated local WAV: captured frames flowed during play, stopped changing while paused, and resumed after play. Both `.qa` APKs were updated in place with `adb install -r`, preserving QA app data; the temporary cache fixture was deleted. The test did not invoke `PlaybackManager` persistence/scrobbling; the user confirmed the local tone was audible from the S24. No permissions or production packages were changed.

## SpotAmp Phase 6 — Collection, Discovery, and Playback Redesign

- **2026-10-21 — S24 completion:** This is SpotAmp Phase 6, separate from SpotCore’s Phase 6. Starting revisions were SpotAmp `d6d84cb6` and SpotCore `041da71d`; all pre-existing working-tree changes were preserved. SpotCore and its v1.4 contract were not changed. Workstreams 1–4 now distinguish saved library metadata from phone audio downloads and provide safe per-item/bulk download controls; preserve shared-playlist conflict handling while adding phone-local station recipes; place Search, recommendations, radio, tuning, and saved stations in Discover with query/results return state; and present the queue as played/current/up-next with reorder, removal, clear-upcoming, playlist save, and persisted playback intent. The Room migration is additive and keeps prior playback state.
- Final same-source gates passed: Quick `20261021T114548279Z-quick` (659 tests / 159 suites, 0 failures); connected S24 UI `20261021T115504236Z-ui` (92 / 23, 0 failures); Release `20261021T115748540Z-release` (unsigned artifact verification, 542 entries). The QA package and instrumentation app were retained, and notification permission cleanup passed.
